The Sweden Democrats' current party programme is based on "democratic [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalism)" and [social conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_conservatism).[^cite_note-autogenerated10-153][^cite_note-\:2-154] The SD platform expresses that the party's core philosophy is inspired by Swedish [national conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism) and parts of the [social democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_democracy) *[folkhemmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folkhemmet)* ('the people's home') idea. SD rejects any positioning of the party within the classic left-right scale, instead referring to itself as a "value-oriented" or "value-conservative" party. The party has stated that "basic [social justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_justice) with traditional conservative ideas", [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalism) and desires for democratic and good governance form its main principles.[^cite_note-auto13-155] Nevertheless, the party is often described by international commentators as being [right-wing](56522.md)[^cite_note-Toemmel2014-30] to [far-right](221220.md).[^cite_note-156]

In policy, SD articulates its main focus to be the areas of immigration, law and order and the elderly. The party also attaches particular importance to its economic and family policies.[^cite_note-157] The SD criticizes [multiculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) in Sweden and emphasizes preserving national heritage. It is also opposed to what it sees as a constant shift of power from Stockholm to the [European Union](9317.md) and campaigns to protect Swedish sovereignty and financial autonomy against the EU.

### Political analysis

The SD's ideology and political identity has evolved significantly in the decades since its founding and entrance into the Riksdag. Labels for the party have consequently been a source of debate by outside observers. Nordic Studies scholar [Benjamin R. Teitelbaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_R._Teitelbaum) called the SD radical nationalist in 2013 and by 2018 said the party has since moved to the "softer side" of European populist parties.[^cite_note-Teitelbaum_2013-78][^cite_note-auto7-158] The party has been described by sociologist [Jens Rydgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Rydgren) and political scientist [Cas Mudde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cas_Mudde) variously as xenophobic, [far-right](221220.md), racist or right-wing populist.[^cite_note-autogenerated10-153][^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] French newspaper *[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde)* profiled the SD as far-right and "a nationalist party with fascist roots".[^cite_note-164] Critics of the SD have argued that it contains a more dangerous or problematic ideology behind a moderate facade,[^cite_note-165] whereas others such as Teitelbaum have argued that research into the SD has been politically biased and that the SD has become more difficult to define due to its beliefs changing over the years.[^cite_note-166] In 2013, a [Sveriges Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sveriges_Radio) journalist called the SD "xenophobic", which resulted in a complaint lodged to the broadcasting regulator by the party. The [Swedish Broadcasting Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Press_and_Broadcasting_Authority) determined that this description was acceptable to use.[^cite_note-167] According to Sveriges Radio in 2017, a European research agency classed the party as "extreme" using the Swedish [GAL-TAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAL-TAN?action=edit\&redlink=1) political scale, arguing that the SD is more traditionalist, socially authoritarian and nationalist and less progressive compared to other Swedish parliamentary parties and described the SD as similar to the [French National Rally](67119.md) in some of its policies.[^cite_note-168] In 2019, the polling company Novus said that their opinion polls show that SD is no-longer a ["one-issue" party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single-issue_politics) in regards to immigration, having won trust in other political questions too.[^cite_note-169] In 2022, University of Gothenburg political scientist Johan Martinsson described the SD as "anti-immigration, anti-multicultural, nationalist" while stopping short of labelling the SD as a far-right party.[^cite_note-bbc.com-170]

However, the characterization of the SD as radical or extreme far-right has come under dispute in recent years by scholars and political observers. Commentator Kateřina Lišaníková observed that the SD had hardline origins through its founders and initial support network, and notes that the SD's leadership openly acknowledges the party's history, but argues the present version of the SD does not match the description of a radical far-right party but is mistakenly labelled as such by media or opponents who focus on the party's early rather than current beliefs. She stated that the SD is now a [national conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism)[^cite_note-Rydgren-8][^cite_note-171] party with populist elements but does not contradict democratic or Swedish constitutional principles.[^cite_note-\:4-172] Similar observations were made in 2021 by Swedish political scientist Sören Holmberg that "extreme right" was not a good description for the SD when placed within the traditional left-right scale, since the party contains centre-left and centrist policies on some issues compared to the other centre-right parties in Sweden. Holmberg furthermore argued that while SD can be considered a [right-wing populist](8354382.md) party, the label "populist" has become unstable due to some of the other parties in the Riksdag adopting populist ideas of their own. He concluded national-conservative was a better term for the SD.[^cite_note-173] Swedish sociologist Göran Adamson has also argued some political opposition conflate the SD's national conservative image as being right-wing extremist, and argued that the SD today is no longer comparable to European extreme-right or neo-fascist parties since the SD has a more liberal direction in several areas, and there is no evidence to suggest that the current incarnation of the party's policies are fascist or anti-democratic.[^cite_note-174]

Describing the party's political trajectory, Johan Martinsson wrote that "culturally nationalist" was the most appropriate label for the Sweden Democrats since the party has continued to change its ideological programs and manifestos since its founding and no longer refer to [ethnic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_nationalism) in party literature. Martinsson acknowledged that the SD had links to Nazi movements during its first years before transforming itself in a conventional political party during the 1990s and since the 2010s the term "radical right" is no longer accurate for the SD. Martinsson summarized that while the SD adopts populist and anti-immigration rhetoric, other parties had also adopted more restrictive stances on migration and the SD's support for welfare state policies contrasts it from other right-leaning parties while the term "right" can be used as a pejorative label as opposed to an accurate description.[^cite_note-175] Sweden-based British journalist Richard Orange noted in 2018 that the SD stands out due to its neo-Nazi roots but in the present now endorses more inclusive "cultural nationalism" over ethnic homogeny while calling for stricter immigration policies.[^cite_note-dw\.com-176] In 2022, British political scientist [Matthew Goodwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Goodwin_\(author\)) described the SD as having transformed itself from an extreme past to becoming part of a broader European family of [national-populist](8354382.md) parties which combine social and cultural conservative nationalism and populism but are opposed to fascist, anti-democratic and revolutionary ideas.[^cite_note-177]

Within the party, the SD's leadership have rejected the extreme and "far-right" labels and argue they no longer represent the party. SD also states that its ideas are not racist and maintains that expressions of neo-fascism or Nazism among its membership are historical and no longer tolerated.[^cite_note-bbc.com-170] [Oscar Sjöstedt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Sj%C3%B6stedt), the SD's financial spokesperson, places the party around the centre on the [left–right political spectrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left%E2%80%93right_political_spectrum),[^cite_note-178] while leader Jimmie Åkesson has stated that they are parallel with the [Moderate Party](202985.md).[^cite_note-179] A 2016 survey of SD MPs in the Riksdag analyzed the party's voting record as closer to the centre-right [Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_\(Sweden\)) on 44 percent of policy issues and closer to the [Red-Greens](20640788.md) on 25 percent of issues.[^cite_note-180] SD group leader [Mattias Karlsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattias_Karlsson_\(politician,_born_1977\)) stated in 2017 that the party is in the middle when it comes to economic redistribution policy and views on the welfare state, but [centre-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-right) on certain values such as defense and immigration policy.[^cite_note-181] Swedish political scientist Henrik Ekengren Oscarsson published a similar conclusion about the party's voter base in 2016 which argued SD supporters are less likely to identify with traditional political labels but align to being centrist or to the left on economic matters and conservative on other issues.[^cite_note-182] In addition, the SD has in recent years increasingly distanced itself from other European ultra-nationalist or far-right parties and stated it does not want anything to do with international and Swedish hard-right entities.[^cite_note-Rydgren-8][^cite_note-183] In spite of this, a 2022 report by Swedish researchers Acta Publica claimed to have found 289 Swedish politicians who expressed racist or neo-Nazi views, with 214 of them being members of the SD.[^cite_note-289nazis-184][^cite_note-nazis-185] The SD itself has argued that some of these controversies with members have been as a result of the party's rapid growth since the late 2000s rather than the party itself being extremist.[^cite_note-SD_launches_an_internal_glossbook-91]

## Policies and beliefs

### Domestic policy

The party is a supporter of the [Swedish monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Sweden) playing a constitutional and cultural role in Swedish life, but also supports an amendment to the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Sweden) that obligates the Riksdag to elect a new monarch in the event of there being no heir to the throne.[^cite_note-186] In 2025 the party supported changing the citizenship requirements to the conditions of being able to speak Swedish, having education about Swedish culture and having an honest way of life, as well as declaring loyalty to Sweden.[^cite_note-187]

The Sweden Democrats have been critical of the special rights given to the indigenous [Sámi people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1mi_people) of northern Sweden. In 2008, the party accepted a motion against the rights to [reindeer husbandry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reindeer_herding). They have argued that those "who do not involve themselves with reindeer husbandry are treated as second class citizens" and that the privileges the herders have are "undemocratic". The party has previously wanted to abolish the [Sámi Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1mi_Parliament_of_Sweden), which it claimed special privileges for an "ethnic minority while the society claims equal rights for others". However, the SD has since disputed that it endorses abolishing the Sámi Parliament with SD spokesman [Mattias Karlsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattias_Karlsson_\(politician,_born_1977\)) saying in a 2013 interview that "the Sami are an indigenous people with an indisputable right to preserve their identity and culture."[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]

### National identity and culture

The Sweden Democrats value a strong common national and [cultural identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_identity), believing these to be the most basic cornerstones of a functioning democracy and successful society. Minimizing linguistic, cultural and religious differences in society and emphasizing a defined sense of Swedish cultural values and social norms has a positive effect on [societal cohesion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_cohesion), maintaining a welfare state and encouraging solidarity among the population, according to the party. On its platform, SD states the Swedish nation is defined "in terms of loyalty, a common language and common culture". A requirement for becoming a member of the Swedish nation is to either "be born in it or by actively choosing to be a part of it". For these reasons among others, SD firmly rejects [multiculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) and argues that Sweden should preserve its cultural heritage by assimilating newcomers and maintaining Swedish as the nation's official language.[^cite_note-\:2-154]

Until the 2000s, the SD attached [ethnopluralist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnopluralism) arguments to its defense of a Swedish homeland and culture.[^cite_note-190] The 2005 party handbook called for a "high degree of ethnic and cultural similarity among the population"[^cite_note-auto13-155] while the old party platform described the need to preserve the "inherited essence" of ethnic Swedes.[^cite_note-191] However, the SD's official platform since 2018 no longer refers to a Swedish nation and people in an ethnically defined context, but adopts a more moderate [cultural conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_conservatism) policy promoting a shared national identity in which foreign-born people can become Swedish through stricter assimilation policies in exchange for citizenship and by the state asserting a more defined concept of Swedish culture and social values.[^cite_note-auto7-158][^cite_note-dw\.com-176] The SD argues that not having a defined sense of what it means to be Swedish makes it harder for those of non-Western cultures to understand and become part of Swedish society. SD representatives have expressed that lacking a defined Swedish cultural identity and society will leave Sweden vulnerable to takeovers by more defined but aggressive cultures and social attitudes, with members of the party citing Islamic culture as an example. The SD has cited a strong work ethic, respect for nature, appreciation for Lutheran Christian traditions, speaking Swedish and equality between the sexes as important parts of Swedish cultural values that the country should strive to maintain.[^cite_note-192] Nevertheless, the idea of a binding "inherited essence" is still a defining feature of SD supporters' worldview. A 2025 study reveals that supporters of the Sweden Democrats are more likely than those of other parties to believe that social differences (e.g., talents, intelligence, behaviours) between individuals, genders, and groups reflect genetic predispositions.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194]

In a 2014 interview for *[Dagens Nyheter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dagens_Nyheter)*, Second Deputy Speaker of the Riksdag and then-party secretary [Björn Söder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_S%C3%B6der) elaborated on the SD party programme with respect to its views on national identity and obtaining Swedish citizenship by saying that although an immigrant of any ethnic background in theory can become a Swedish citizen, they would have to adapt and be [assimilated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_assimilation) in order to be considered Swedish in the cultural sense, since the SD does not view a simple acquisition of Swedish citizenship with no assimilation requirements as the same as becoming part of Swedish society.[^cite_note-Den_leende_nationalismen-195][^cite_note-196] In the interview, Söder stated that the officially recognised Swedish minority peoples such as [Sámi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1mi_peoples), [Tornedalians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornedalians) and [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Sweden) in many cases had dual cultural identities and that they probably would be proud of both heritages, and claimed that while these minorities are part of Sweden today, they had to give up part of their own culture in the past to integrate and become culturally Swedish.[^cite_note-Den_leende_nationalismen-195] It was widely interpreted that Söder had stated in the interview that Jews cannot be Swedish unless they abandon their Jewish identity.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198] Söder's comments were accused of being antisemitic and caused Swedish parliamentary groups and party leaders to call for his resignation.[^cite_note-199] The [Simon Wiesenthal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center) listed the statement as number six on their list of the top ten most anti-semitic events of 2014.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] Söder responded in an article for *[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post)*, denying the charges of anti-semitism and claiming *Dagens Nyheter* had taken his statements out of context. In response, the SD also stated that it rejects antisemitism in Sweden and supports Israel in foreign policy, and that its proposed assimilation model is based on recognizing the difference between the concept of nationhood and acquired citizenship as opposed to implying minorities have split loyalties.[^cite_note-203]

The Sweden Democrats advocates a cultural policy that would strip government funding for multicultural initiatives and strengthen support for traditional Swedish culture. This agenda has often manifested itself as opposition to state funding of immigrant cultural organisations and festivals, and more support for traditional Swedish craft, folk music, and folk dance groups. The party also tends to oppose state support for cultural initiatives deemed provocative or elitist.[^cite_note-Teitelbaum_2013-78] A 2014 letter signed by 52 Swedish anthropologists, criticised the Sweden Democrats' use of the terms "culture" (*kultur* [\[kɵlˈtʉːr\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Swedish) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/b/b8/Sv-Kultur.ogg/Sv-Kultur.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/b/b8/Sv-Kultur.ogg/Sv-Kultur.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>) and "anthropology" (*antropologi* [\[antrɔpʊlʊˈɡiː\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Swedish) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/91/Sv-Antropologi.ogg/Sv-Antropologi.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/91/Sv-Antropologi.ogg/Sv-Antropologi.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>), claiming their views on culture were "[essentialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_essentialism) and obsolete", clarifying that culture is "dynamic" and "in constant change".[^cite_note-204] The Sweden Democrats themselves maintain that traditional elements of Sweden's culture should not be rendered obsolete in favour of multiculturalism and argue Swedish cultural values contributed to the nation's economic and social successes through the "people's home" (*[Folkhemmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folkhemmet)*) concept. According to scholar Bo Nilsson, the party links the adoption of multicultural policies with a subsequent decline in the *Folkhemmet* model and Sweden's success.[^cite_note-205]

The party has expressed opposition to certain forms of modern architecture and in 2022 called for new housing to reflect traditional architectural styles, with the SD supporting more investment and streamlined planning permission rules into building new homes designed to match the specifications of what the party calls the "Sverigehuset" (the Sweden House), a classic wooden house design in Sweden. In 2025, the SD proposed to the National Board of Housing, Building and Planning to build more affordable housing in traditional designs.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

The Sweden Democrats also criticise modern art, and have accused local councils of wasting public money on what it calls "provocative" art.[^cite_note-208] The SD want citizens to be able to vote in local referendums on public art displayed near schools, public transport stations and town centres. "The important thing is that what is expressed in the public environment is anchored to the citizens and especially the local residents who are most often in the environment so that they feel an identification", says the party's cultural spokesperson [Aron Emilsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aron_Emilsson).[^cite_note-209] Sweden Democrats mayor in [Sölvesborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%B6lvesborg) [Louise Erixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louise_Erixon) claimed "There's a big division between what the general public thinks is beautiful and interesting and what a tiny cultural elite thinks is exciting".[^cite_note-210]

The Sweden Democrats also support a ban on the [burqa](165826.md) and [niqāb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niq%C4%81b) in public places and are against proposals to publicly broadcast the Islamic call to prayer from [minarets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minaret).[^cite_note-211] The SD wants tougher enforcement of existing laws against [female genital mutilation](11408.md), honor violence and social segregation. The party also wants Swedish to remain Sweden's main official language in state funded schools, government agencies and public funded media, and for more teaching of Swedish cultural history in schools.[^cite_note-212] It also supports prohibiting the [hijab](68301.md) in primary schools, arguing that while it is not opposed to hijabs in general, the choice to wear it should be made on an individual basis when a child reaches adulthood.[^cite_note-213] The SD is strongly opposed to [sharia law](28840.md) being incorporated into the Swedish legal system.[^cite_note-brookings-214]

Leading party representatives have also spoken out in various contexts against mosques and Islamic centres in Sweden.[^cite_note-215] In 2023, SD leader [Jimmie Åkesson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmie_%C3%85kesson) argued that Swedish mosques which preach "anti-democratic, anti-Swedish, homophobic, anti-Semitic propaganda" should be closed and demolished and expressed opposition to the construction of new Islamic buildings. SD has also argued that other Swedish parties, particularly those on the left such as the [Social Democrats](202978.md) are tolerant of Islamist activities in Sweden to secure votes at the expense of Swedish culture and security.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218][^cite_note-brookings-214] In January 2024, [Richard Jomshof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Jomshof), chairman of the Justice Committee in the Riksdag, ignited controversy in Sweden by proposing the prohibition of the Islamic [star and crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_and_crescent).[^cite_note-219] He drew parallels to the ban on the [Swastika](45943.md), claiming that both symbols represent something dangerous.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]

#### Immigration

The Sweden Democrats believe that [Sweden's recent immigration](24537353.md) and integration policies have been a national failure. In a statement filed before the [Riksdag](158651.md) Committee on Migration in August 2020, SD claims that Sweden's "irresponsible" immigration and asylum policies have subjected Sweden to an on-going "long-term, albeit low-intensity crisis".[^cite_note-\:1-222] The SD official policy brief states that the party "welcomes those who contribute to our \[Sweden's] society, who follow our laws and respect our customs. On the other hand, anyone who comes here and exploits our systems, commits crimes or exposes our citizens to danger is not welcome".[^cite_note-223]

Historically, SD sought to repatriate most immigrants and ban immigration entirely; however, these policies were moderated in the 1990s before being abolished altogether.[^cite_note-auto6-77] Presently, SD positions itself against [mass immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_immigration) and wishes to strongly restrict immigration by instead giving generous support to those who do not want to assimilate into Swedish society to emigrate back to their country of origin. The SD also wants to change laws to allow for wider deportations of foreign residents who engage in illegal activity or are unemployed and abusing state welfare.[^cite_note-224] The Sweden Democrats also call for an expansion of circumstances in which citizenship can be revoked from naturalized citizens, with Jimmie Åkesson mentioning examples of those who commit crimes, engage in delinquency, support terrorism or remain socially unintegrated into society.[^cite_note-225] The Sweden Democrats also support raising the [remigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remigration) allowance given to migrants and asylum seekers who seek to voluntarily leave the country.[^cite_note-226] The SD argues that its immigration policies are not based on xenophobia and that the party is not opposed to immigration per se, but believe immigration must stay at a level where it does not "threaten national identity, the country's welfare or security".[^cite_note-227] SD has also campaigned to restrict immigration from what it calls "culturally distant" countries and argues that temporary work visas should be limited only to skills that are impossible to find in Sweden.[^cite_note-228] SD are against [free movement of labour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_movement_for_workers_in_the_European_Union) within the European Union, calling on Sweden to revise its membership of the [Schengen agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schengen_agreement), but support free movement between [Nordic nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries).[^cite_note-auto13-155] The SD believes that Sweden will be better able to help [refugees](45547.md) and economic migrants in their home areas once more state funds are freed from being directed towards funding immigration in Sweden.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-auto1-230] SD also favours cultural assimilation over integration of immigrants from non-Western backgrounds, arguing that integration is a meet in the middle approach and that Swedes should not have to bear the burden over what the party claims have been reckless immigration policies. [Torbjörn Kastell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torbj%C3%B6rn_Kastell?action=edit\&redlink=1) (former party secretary from 2003 to 2004) said in 2002 that the party wanted "a multicultural world, not a multicultural society".[^cite_note-autogenerated10-153][^cite_note-231]

When handling asylum seekers and refugee cases, the SD supports protecting [national sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation_state) in regards to Sweden's decisions on migration and border control. The party calls for Sweden to adopt "the principle of first safe country" into its asylum laws, meaning that asylum seekers should only be able to seek refugee status in the first safe country that they arrive in and will be refused refugee status in Sweden if they have already crossed multiple safe countries or their case is already registered in a foreign state.[^cite_note-232] Until such legislation is realized by the Riksdag, SD supports implementing stronger deterrents against asylum migration to Sweden through other countries and setting stricter limits on the right to welfare that asylum seekers are entitled to by the Swedish state and making cultural assimilation of those already in Sweden mandatory. The party opposes offering [permanent residency](1284324.md) to refugees, believing that temporary residency should be the standard for those who claim asylum in Sweden before a path to full residency or citizenship is considered. SD supports Sweden eventually accepting refugees exclusively through the [UNHCR](31964.md) resettlement programme in accordance with a quota based on each [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Sweden)'s capacity once Sweden's asylum laws are reformed and tightened.[^cite_note-\:1-222][^cite_note-233] SD has also referred to the recommendations from the [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md) (UNHCR), which state that the return of refugees should be the solution to refugee problems.[^cite_note-autogenerated10-153] On asylum cases from the Middle East and Africa, the party supports giving priority to persecuted Christian, former Muslim and other religious or sexual minorities fleeing war or death for apostasy, believing that such individuals are less likely to be offered refuge elsewhere.[^cite_note-auto4-234] In 2017, members of the Sweden Democrats' leadership defended comments made by then US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in response to Trump's assertion that Sweden's immigration and asylum policies had led to a rise of terrorism and crime in Sweden.[^cite_note-235] However, following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) the SD supported accepting and accommodating Ukrainian refugees in Sweden.[^cite_note-rmx.news-236] Ahead of the [2022 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Swedish_general_election), the party campaigned to tighten the rules in the Swedish Aliens Act ([Utlänningslagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utl%C3%A4nningslagen?action=edit\&redlink=1)) to the strictest possible level within European law and encourage voluntary [remigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remigration) of asylum seekers and immigrants who are economically inactive or remain culturally unassimilated.[^cite_note-auto1-230]

The SD also wants European governments to construct a security wall along the European border with Turkey in response to illegal immigration, terrorism and incursions by the [Grey Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_Wolves_\(organization\)) into Greece. It also calls on the European Union to commonly adopt a migration system based on the [Australian model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Sovereign_Borders) to prevent human trafficking across the Mediterranean, which the party states enables illegal immigrants and terrorists to reach Sweden.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] SD calls for compulsory measures for immigrants to be employed, live and work in Sweden for at least ten years, learn the [Swedish language](26689.md), go through a mandatory cultural assimilation program, and be subject to a language and social skills test before becoming eligible for Swedish citizenship.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-auto1-230] The party also supports increased spending on border patrol forces, [zero tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero_tolerance) laws on illegal immigration and foreign-born criminals, repatriations of [Schengen Area](16565139.md) migrants who move to Sweden to make a living from street begging, changes in the law to enable the government to strip foreign-born Swedish nationals of their citizenship if found guilty of a crime, penalties against employers who use foreign and undocumented labor to circumvent Swedish working conditions and stricter laws against family migration.[^cite_note-auto1-230]

In recent years, the SD has tried to approach the immigration policies of the [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party), which from 2001 to 2011 provided parliamentary support for the former Danish centre-right governments in return for a tightening of Danish immigration policies and stricter naturalisation laws.[^cite_note-240] Following the [2022 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Swedish_general_election), the Sweden Democrats achieved this objective under the [Tidö Agreement](72007581.md) with the centre-right Moderate, Christian Democrat and Liberal Party coalition government.[^cite_note-241] In exchange for SD parliamentary support, the Swedish government conceded to some of the SD's immigration policy demands on requirements for obtaining Swedish citizenship and expanding the deportation circumstances for foreign-born residents and asylum seekers.[^cite_note-242]

Following scenes of people in Swedish cities celebrating the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), the SD leadership stated that those who praised the attack should be expelled from the country and that the Tidö Agreement clause on deporting non-citizens on grounds of poor character and not just criminal convictions should be used against those who support [Hamas](13913.md). SD also states that Swedes of foreign backgrounds who leave the country to join legally designated terrorist groups should be stripped of their citizenship or residency and denied re-entry into the country.[^cite_note-243]

During the [2026 Swedish general election](72049993.md) campaign, Åkesson warned in the final debate in [SVT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sveriges_Television) that practicing Muslims, who visit mosques, can decide the election result. He argued that both the [Swedish Social Democratic Party](202978.md) and the [Left Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left_Party_\(Sweden\)) have strategies of mobilizing in mosques and Muslim congregations to gain voters.[^cite_note-244] He was subsequently heavily criticized by the opposition and by the editorial of *[Dagens Nyheter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dagens_Nyheter)* for not accepting that every individual vote is of equal value.[^cite_note-245]

### Society and crime

#### Family policy

The Sweden Democrats consider children raised in a [nuclear family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_family) as the preferred option for the child's development. Those not raised by their biological parents should have the right to associate with or at least find out who they were. SD has been critical of adoption and [artificial insemination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_insemination) for same-sex couples and [polyamorous](24591.md) people.[^cite_note-246]

The party also calls for a ban on child, polygamous and [forced marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_marriage), as well as harsher penalties for [honor violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honor_killing). It also supports a zero-tolerance stance against [female genital mutilation](11408.md) within Sweden and abroad, and for perpetrators to be prosecuted or if necessary deported.[^cite_note-247] SD also wants certain restrictions on [male circumcision](8718425.md), calling for a ban on minors unless its for medical reasons. The party says that while it does support male circumcision for religious reasons, it should be performed at the age of consent and the state should not fund it through the healthcare system.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249]

SD supports [abortion](765.md) being legal in Sweden and free access to abortions up to the legal limit in Sweden which is 22 weeks since 2019.[^cite_note-250]

#### LGBT rights

The SD has changed and moderated its position on LGBT in Swedish society over the years, currently saying it opposes discrimination on grounds of sexual orientation. Historically, members of SD have criticized a so-called "[Homosex Lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_Mafia)" while claiming they were not hostile to homosexuals and previously opposed government sanctioned adoption to single people and same-sex couples, unless the adopting party are close relatives or already have a close relationship with the child. However, the party has since shifted its stance to supporting [same-sex adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_adoption) and supports privately funded insemination for single or gay parents. Party leader Jimmie Åkesson has expressed concerns that what he describes as the gradual [Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spread_of_Islam) of Sweden will eventually lead to the rights of [sexual minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_minority) being violated.[^cite_note-251] The SD supports legal recognition of [same-sex marriage in Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Sweden) and [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union) for same-sex couples but argues the church should have the final say on performing the wedding ceremony over the state.[^cite_note-252]

Until the early 2000s, *SD-Kuriren* (the official SD party newspaper) regularly published articles criticizing LGBT events and describing homosexuality as "perversion", before moderating itself alongside a shift in party ideology.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256] A blog post claiming [Stockholm Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm_Pride) sexualised young children and [equating homosexuality with pedophilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Societal_attitudes_toward_homosexuality#Association_with_child_sexual_abuse_and_pedophilia) titled *Botten måste snart vara nådd* ("Soon enough we'll hit rock bottom") was published by SD Party secretary [Björn Söder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_S%C3%B6der) on 1 August 2007.[^cite_note-257] The post was widely criticised in the Swedish media as an attack on LGBT people.[^cite_note-258]

An unofficial [pride parade](58168.md) called Pride Järva was organised by SD member and former party magazine editor Jan Sjunnesson in the Stockholm suburbs of [Tensta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tensta) and [Husby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Husby,_Stockholm), two areas with large immigrant populations.[^cite_note-259] The event was disavowed by the official Stockholm Pride organisation and the [Swedish Federation for Lesbian, Gay, Bisexual and Transgender Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Federation_for_Lesbian,_Gay,_Bisexual_and_Transgender_Rights); in a joint statement, both organisations called Sjunnesson "a person who's spreading hatred towards Muslims on social media \[and] who's not supporting LGBT rights".[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] Approximately 30 people participated in Pride Järva, with a larger amount of LGBT and heterosexual anti-racist [counter-protestors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-protest) arriving to oppose them.[^cite_note-vice_jarva_pride-262] In 2014, the official [Stockholm Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm_Pride) voted to ban the SD from participating that year, which was met with criticism from both within the party and from some opposition politicians, who argued it was undemocratic.[^cite_note-263]

In recent years, the SD has shifted its stance to being more supportive of [LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_by_country_or_territory#Northern_Europe) and same-sex parenting and marriage by updating and expanding its policies regarding LGBT issues. In 2010, SD leader Jimmie Åkesson and party vice-president [Carina Ståhl Herrstedt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carina_St%C3%A5hl_Herrstedt) published an article apologizing for past [homophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobic) statements made by party members, and arguing that mass immigration risked eroding the rights of Sweden's gay community.[^cite_note-264] In its current platform, the SD states "everyone must be treated equally, regardless of sexual orientation, and discrimination must be combated".[^cite_note-auto11-265][^cite_note-266] In a 2018 interview, SD member of the Riksdag and gender-equality spokesperson [Paula Bieler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paula_Bieler) stated that homophobes "are not welcome in our party".[^cite_note-267] Since 2018, the SD party program has been updated to support same-sex adoption and parenting.[^cite_note-268] In 2025, the SD proposed constructing a statue in [Örebro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%96rebro), dedicated to [Muhsin Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhsin_Hendricks), the world's first openly gay imam, who had been shot dead in his car in South Africa, to honour his work for the LGBTQI movement, as well as constructing a statue in [Rinkeby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rinkeby) to honour the rights of LGBTQI people in the area.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

The SD supports [gender-affirming surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-affirming_surgery), as long as the motive behind it is mental wellbeing, and permission is given by a medical professional.[^cite_note-271]

Gender studies scholars Karlberg, Korolczuk and Sältenberg note that politicians representing the Sweden Democrats are among "the primary actors advancing [anti-gender discourses" in Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-gender_movement_in_Sweden), a discourse they argue contributes to an "insidious [de-democratization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De-democratization)" by targeting vulnerable groups.[^cite_note-Karlberg-23]

#### Gender equality

SD opposes any "negative or positive special treatment on the basis of gender, age, sexual orientation, nationality or ethnic origin" in the labour market.[^cite_note-\:3-272]

The party maintains that, collectively, there are biological differences between men and women, some of which that cannot be "observed with the naked eye". Perceived differences between men and women in regards to preference, behaviour and life choices exist due to each individual's choices and does not necessarily have to be "problematic, the result of discrimination nor the result of an oppressive gender power structure".[^cite_note-\:3-272]

#### Church policies

The party believes that the [Church of Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Sweden) should have a special position in Swedish society. In order to protect Swedish cultural heritage, members of the party want to protect the Lutheran tradition in the church elections and counteract secular party-political control of the Swedish Church. SD supports interfaith dialogue but opposes Qur'an recitals or imams delivering Islamic services in churches which the SD claims to be part of "Islamizing Swedish society".[^cite_note-273] The SD previously called for the Church of Sweden to be reinstated as a [state church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_church) but no longer supports this policy, however they continue to support holidays in connection with religious occasions to only include traditionally Swedish and Christian holidays with party leader Jimmie Åkesson stating in a 2014 interview that he wants to protect Christianity as a "system of norms".[^cite_note-dagen.se-274] In Swedish Church Assembly election campaigns, the SD has campaigned on the issue of Christians who are subjected to persecution and hate crimes in Sweden and abroad.[^cite_note-dagen.se-274][^cite_note-275]

#### Law and order and security

The Sweden Democrats support generally increased [minimum sentences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_sentence) as well as increased resources and personnel for the [police](47359232.md). SD wishes to instate the possibility of [life imprisonment without parole](148681.md) for the worst crimes and to repatriate foreign citizens found guilty of serious crimes.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] SD also wants to establish a public register of individuals convicted of certain sexual crimes.[^cite_note-278] The party also supports increased surveillance of those known to be involved in criminal gangs and terrorism. SD argues for a zero-tolerance law for people who physically attack police officers and emergency workers.[^cite_note-279]

SD previously supported the reinstatement of [capital punishment](5902.md) before dropping it as an official policy after the party program was updated in 1998, although individuals within the party continue to support the death penalty for serious crimes such as murder and [infanticide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infanticide) and have called for [chemical castration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_castration) of convicted child sexual abusers.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282]

The SD opposes allowing Swedish citizens who joined [ISIS](9087364.md) to return to Sweden and argues that anyone who joins a foreign terrorist group should be denied state funded assistance. In 2023, SD also stated that Swedes who have travelled to the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) to support Hamas should be banned from reentering the country.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] SD has also emphasised a desire to crack down on abuses and crimes of which the elderly are particular targets.[^cite_note-\:9-285]

In 2025, the SD supported passing stricter ownership regulations on [AR-15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AR-15) rifles following the [2025 Risbergska school shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Risbergska_school_shooting) although some senior party spokespeople expressed opposition to amending gun ownership laws citing the impact it could have on farmers and hunters.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]

### Economy

The Sweden Democrats have described themselves as supporters of the [Swedish welfare state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_in_Sweden), [labour rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_rights) and the [public sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_sector), but argue that welfare should be restricted to Swedish citizens and permanent residents.[^cite_note-288] The party argues that foreign-born nationals must show proof of legal residence, paid taxes and financial self-support for a certain period to become eligible for welfare.[^cite_note-289] In its platform, the SD claims that its economic policies are neither left or right-wing, but designed to improve conditions for [small and medium-sized companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_and_medium-sized_business), [self-employed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-employed) citizens and [entrepreneurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship) to boost employment and stimulate the economy, as opposed to what it describes as "constructed jobs" created by the state to reduce unemployment but hold no long term benefit for the Swedish economy or career paths for the people who work them. SD wants to abolish the Swedish Employment Service in its current form and replace it with a new authority for the supervision and close regulation of private employment services to ensure large corporations do not exploit or undercut Swedish workers. The party supports affordable and free access to public healthcare for Swedish nationals.[^cite_note-290]

SD supports certain [free trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade) conditions but believe Sweden must exit or revise trade agreements that pose a threat to Sweden's sovereignty and Swedish workers. The party favours certain measures of [economic protectionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism) and support state-ownership of companies that operate Swedish mines, agricultural land and produce energy or defense equipment. However, SD also support abolishing [inheritance tax](5824627.md) and reducing [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax).[^cite_note-auto13-155] Since the 2010s, the SD has been critical of Chinese government involvement in infrastructure projects and trade deals with Sweden.[^cite_note-291] SD wishes to lower the tax rate for the elderly, as well as increase subsidised housing for the elderly.[^cite_note-\:9-285] SD also wishes to allocate additional resources to municipalities in order to provide seniors with greater food assistance and, in general, improve their quality of life.[^cite_note-\:9-285]

Political author Anders Backlund described the party as "economically centrist", leaning towards [economic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_nationalism) (in contrast to the other Swedish conservative parties who tend to favour open free markets and global cosmopolitan philosophies) and supporting a [mixed market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy) combining centre-left and centre-right ideas, as well as promoting "[welfare chauvinist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_chauvinism)" policies which blend national-populism with socio-economics.[^cite_note-292] According to political scientist Johan Martinsson: "In economic terms, the party is more centrist and pragmatic, with a mixture of left and right-wing proposals".[^cite_note-293]

### Environment

The party argues that, while Sweden should maintain its "active role in global climate cooperation", other countries should reduce their emissions, as it believes Sweden to already be doing enough on that front.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-\:8-295] The party opposed the [Paris agreement](30242372.md),[^cite_note-\:8-295] and advocates keeping [nuclear power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_Sweden) as a prominent energy source in Sweden,[^cite_note-296] believing it to be an efficient way to [mitigate climate change](2119179.md). They also advocate investing in climate research internationally and funding climate action on a global scale.[^cite_note-auto11-265]

#### Agriculture and forestry

SD supports creating a specialist law enforcement branch for animal welfare and call for stricter penalties against animal cruelty. In 2017, the SD members of the Riksdag Justice Committee filed a motion calling for extended prison sentences for those found guilty of animal abuse, which was ultimately supported by the other parties.[^cite_note-297] The party opposes EU influence over domestic laws on hunting, farming and forestry within Sweden, calling for such policies to be decided upon by rural Swedes instead of Brussels. SD is also critical of non-stun animal slaughter and want to ban the import of goods that contradict Swedish animal welfare legislation.[^cite_note-298]

### Foreign policy

#### Defense

SD wants to increase the number of [Swedish Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Army) brigades to seven from 2021's two.[^cite_note-\:3-272] The party has stated that it would seek to raise Sweden's [defense spending](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_spending) to 2–2.5% of GDP.[^cite_note-newsnowfinland.fi-299][^cite_note-\:3-272]

The party supports closer military cooperation with neighboring [Nordic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries) and previously opposed Swedish membership of [NATO](21133.md), instead calling for an alignment without full membership.[^cite_note-newsnowfinland.fi-299] However, following the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) the SD leadership announced it would consider changing its policy to endorse NATO membership and support joining if [Finland](10577.md) also applied for NATO membership.[^cite_note-300] It subsequently voted in favor for NATO membership in the Riksdag.[^cite_note-301]

#### European Union

The Sweden Democrats support close political co-operation within the [Nordic sphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries), but are [Eurosceptic](10037.md) and against further [EU integration](683704.md) and transfers of Swedish sovereignty to the [European Union](9317.md).[^cite_note-auto2-302] The party states that it supports European political and economic co-operation to secure trade and combat cross-border organized crime, illegal immigration, [Islamism](15012.md), terrorism and environmental challenges but strongly opposes creating an [EU army](1074631.md) or policies that could lead to the creation of a [Federal European Superstate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Europe).[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-auto8-304][^cite_note-auto2-302] The party generally opposes EU regulation over Swedish tax, pensions, asylum policy, environmental and domestic affairs and calls for the national sovereignty and cultural identity of European nations to be prioritized above the EU's political ambitions.[^cite_note-305] SD rejects joining the [Economic and Monetary Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_and_Monetary_Union_of_the_European_Union) by opposing the [Euro](9472.md) currency and favors keeping the [krona](16709.md). They also seek to reduce Swedish financial contributions to Brussels, end Swedish participation in bailouts for other EU member states, renegotiate Swedish membership of the [Schengen Agreement](179592.md) and opt-out of common asylum agreements, protect freedom of speech and the [free access to the internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_Internet_access) from EU copyright bills, and are against the [accession of Turkey to the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union).[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-auto2-302]

The party has called for Sweden to renegotiate its membership of the EU and seeks an amendment to the Swedish constitution to make it mandatory that proposed EU treaties and financial transfers be first put to a public vote.[^cite_note-307] The SD believes that if the EU cannot be reformed or attempts to transform itself into a Superstate, Sweden must immediately reconsider its membership via a referendum and prepare to leave the EU.[^cite_note-auto4-234] The SD's youth-wing, the [Young Swedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Swedes_SDU) (*Ungsvenskarna*), support a Swedish exit from the EU. In 2023, party leader Åkesson and SD European Union spokesman [Charlie Weimers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Weimers) unveiled a new EU strategy with the objective of achieving better negotiation results, increasing Swedish influence and stopping the EU from gaining more power by creating a [referendum lock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Act_2011) based on the [UK](31717.md) [European Union Act 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Act_2011) in the Riksdag to prevent transfer of powers to Brussels without a mandatory public vote. SD argues that a referendum lock should be used to prevent further [EU expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU_expansion) to include Turkey, Albania and Balkan states.[^cite_note-308] The SD has also said it wants a Swedish exit from the EU be made possible by removing all references to membership in the constitution and would appoint a panel of experts to determine the best strategy to leave the EU.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] In 2025, the Sweden Democrats stated that while they remain critical of the EU, they believe EU funds are necessary and pragmatic in the current climate for European military rearmament, with the party citing the war in Ukraine and deteriorating security situation between the United States and Europe.[^cite_note-\:5-312][^cite_note-\:11-34][^cite_note-313]

#### Russia

The SD has taken a strongly pro-Ukraine position following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md) and has called on Sweden and Western governments to help the Ukrainian people defend their homeland.[^cite_note-rmx.news-236] Åkesson said in 2024 that the SD stands fully behind Sweden's support for Ukraine, and that they would continue to do so "as long as the Ukrainian people fight their heroic fight".[^cite_note-314] He also said that sees no limit on how much the EU should send to Ukraine, adding that he supports a [future Ukrainian membership in the EU](5612265.md).[^cite_note-315] In 2022, an analysis of votes relating to Russia in the [European Parliament](9581.md) found that the SD were the 10th-most critical party in the parliament, having voted against Russian interests 93 percent of the time.[^cite_note-316] The report found that—among all Swedish parties—the SD were the most critical of Russia.[^cite_note-317] Following the Russian invasion, the party got rid of members who had previously expressed support for Putin.[^cite_note-318]

In 2025 and 2026, the SD continued to support increased Swedish military and civilian assistance to Ukraine and measures aimed at strengthening Sweden's defence against Russia. In January 2025, the government and the SD presented Sweden's largest military support package to Ukraine at the time, worth SEK 13.5 billion, marking a shift towards procuring newly produced military equipment for Ukraine.[^cite_note-319] In September 2025, the government and the SD agreed to increase the framework for military support to Ukraine to SEK 40 billion annually for 2026 and 2027.[^cite_note-320] The party has continued to describe support for Ukraine as important for Sweden's and Europe's security, with Åkesson reiterating the party's support for Ukraine in 2025.[^cite_note-321] In February 2026, the government and the SD also presented a further military support package worth SEK 12.9 billion, including funding for long-range drones, ammunition, and air defence.[^cite_note-322]

Ahead of the [2026 Swedish general election](72049993.md), the SD faced scrutiny over alleged pro-Russian links. In September 2026, the anti-racism magazine *[Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expo_\(magazine\))* reported that Polina Malaberg, a political staffer for the SD in the Riksdag, had for years shared pro-Kremlin narratives on social media and had participated in a Russia-linked network, including a 2017 trip to [Moscow](19004.md) connected to a Russian influence operation.[^cite_note-323] Malaberg is also the partner of Gustav Gellerbrant, head of the SD's coordination office with the government and a key figure in the [Tidö Agreement](72007581.md).[^cite_note-324] Åkesson described the allegations as serious and said they would be investigated, while Gellerbrant subsequently took parental leave and surrendered his access card and computer. The affair prompted renewed criticism from the opposition over possible Russian influence and security risks within the party's political network.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326]

#### Middle East

The Sweden Democrats are supportive of [Israel](9282173.md). The party favors recognising [Jerusalem](16043.md) as Israel's capital and proposes moving the [Swedish embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Sweden) there.[^cite_note-327] A study by the *European Coalition for Israel* documented that SD had the most pro-Israel voting record of the Swedish parties in the European Parliament.[^cite_note-auto12-328] In 2021, the Israeli government stated that they did not maintain relations with the SD due to "its roots in Nazism"[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] but by 2023 had dropped its non-cooperation stance after SD representatives signed a document of principle with Israeli ministers pledging to combat antisemitism. Between 2023 and 2024, a delegation of senior SD members visited Israel to hold meetings with [Knesset](74656.md) politicians and discuss a cooperation pact with the [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) party.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] The party has also taken a strongly pro-Israel position following the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), supporting military action to remove Hamas and calling on the Swedish government to review all funding to Palestinian organizations which the SD accuse of spending aid money on terrorism.[^cite_note-auto12-328]

SD has advocated a "neutral" position on the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) and sent a delegation to meet with Syrian officials in 2017.[^cite_note-333]

#### Other regions

In 2011, SD was the only Swedish political party to vote against Swedish involvement in the [2011 military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya).[^cite_note-334] SD also supports the creation of an [independent Kurdish state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan) and for the [Armenian genocide](140376.md) to be formally recognised by the international community.[^cite_note-auto4-234] In 2019, SD-member [Björn Söder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_S%C3%B6der) received the [Mkhitar Gosh Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orders,_decorations,_and_medals_of_Armenia#Mkhitar_Gosh_Medal) by President [Armen Sarksyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armen_Sarksyan) and [Markus Wiechel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Wiechel) received the Medal of Honour of the [National Assembly of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Armenia). These were the first Sweden Democrats to receive international awards for their political engagement. Since 2022, the SD has participated in [bipartisan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bipartisan) efforts to give more diplomatic recognition to [Taiwan](25734.md), and supported a motion in the Riksdag to change the name of Sweden's representative office in Taiwan to "House of Sweden". SD politician [Markus Wiechel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Wiechel) said the name change denotes that Sweden should treat Taiwan as a nation rather than a disputed territory or a province of China.[^cite_note-335] Wiechel also proposed giving military support to Taiwan and said SD sees Taiwan as an independent state.[^cite_note-336] Three SD-members of Parliament ([Markus Wiechel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Wiechel), [Björn Söder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_S%C3%B6der) and [Nima Gholam Ali Pour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nima_Gholam_Ali_Pour)) received "Certificate of Appreciation", an award from Taipei Mission in Sweden because of their commitment to strengthen Swedish-Taiwanese relations. SD politician and MEP [Charlie Weimers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Weimers) also enhanced Taiwan-EU relations through his motion, which got approval in the [European Parliament](9581.md) on closer cooperation between Taiwan and EU.[^cite_note-337]

## Organization

### Leadership

#### Party leaders

| Party leader | Time in office |  |
| --- | --- | --- |

### Internal structure

The Sweden Democrats are made up of 16 districts of local party associations with executive boards. Each district consists of a number of municipal associations, which may include one or more municipalities. In municipalities that are not covered by a municipal association, the party organises its members as *working groups* instead. The SD also has a centralized national board permanently chaired by the party leader and party secretary and whose other members are elected by the SD's membership base.[^cite_note-339]

Within the SD there is a women's branch [SD-Women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SD-Women)[^cite_note-340] and an affiliated youth-wing [Young Swedes SDU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Swedes_SDU) which was founded in 2015.[^cite_note-341] The SD's first youth-wing was founded in 1993 as the *Sweden Democratic Youth Association* before it was renamed the [Sweden Democratic Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_Democratic_Youth) (SDU). The old SDU was disbanded in 1995 due to extremism problems before it was reconstituted in 1998. Many prominent SD politicians including party leader Jimmie Åkesson were members of the SDU. In 2015, the SD announced it would expel the leadership of the SDU from the mother party and officially sever ties with it due to ongoing controversies with its members. The party subsequently created the Young Swedes SDU as a replacement.[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343]

Following the 2010 Swedish general election, the SD created its own security unit which by 2014 consisted of an estimated 60 people. SD states that the unit is intended to handle internal issues within the party, including cybersecurity, to marshal public events and to encourage members to report external threats to the police.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-Åkesson_kritisk-345] The SD has argued the security wing is necessary due to threats against SD politicians and highlighted a 2012 report by the [Swedish National Council for Crime Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_National_Council_for_Crime_Prevention) which found one in two of every local SD politician had experienced some form of threat, harassment or physical violence, and that SD legislators were statistically twice as more likely to be threatened than members of other parties. SD reported 95 instances of threats or violence against elected officials to the police and Riksdag security in 2012.[^cite_note-Åkesson_kritisk-345][^cite_note-346]

### Associated organisations and media

Since its founding, the SD has published its own newspaper *SD-Kuriren* which was previously known as *Sverige-Kuriren* and then *SD Bulletin* until 2003. Party secretary [Richard Jomshof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Jomshof) currently serves as the paper's editor.[^cite_note-347] In 2014, the party also launched an online magazine *Samtiden* ('Contemporary'). It is currently edited by Swedish economist [Dick Erixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Erixon).[^cite_note-348]

In 2020, [Mattias Karlsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattias_Karlsson_\(politician,_born_1977\)), the former group leader of the Sweden Democrats in the Riksdag founded [Oikos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oikos_\(Swedish_think-tank\)), a conservative think-tank. Expo has alleged the think-tank to be an "extension of the Sweden Democrats' political project" supposedly also receiving funding from the party[^cite_note-Poohl-134] although the group itself claims to be non-partisan.[^cite_note-\:0-349]

In 2020, the party also helped to launch a web based TV channel called Riks,[^cite_note-350] through their wholly owned online magazine *Samtiden*, with the ambition that the media channel should not be an official party TV.[^cite_note-351] However, in a 2024 investigatory exposé conducted by [TV4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV4_\(Swedish_TV_channel\))’s investigatory branch, [Kalla fakta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalla_fakta?action=edit\&redlink=1), it was discovered that Riks and the SD are in a close-knit relationship with each other. For example, the exposé showed that Riks rents its office spaces from the Sweden Democrats, and that members of the SD and Riks come into frequent contact with one another, moving freely between each other's offices. Moreover, the SD's communications department instructed Riks to hide any connection to SD before an *[Expressen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressen)* interview with Dick Erixon in Riks' offices.[^cite_note-352] In addition to that, the programme showed that the SD's communications department used anonymous social media accounts to artificially disseminate Riks' posts and [YouTube](3524766.md) videos.[^cite_note-ISTroll-1][^cite_note-TrollFabrikTV4-2]

### Voter demography

According to [Statistics Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_Sweden)'s (SCB) 2017 party preference survey, the Sweden Democrats (SD) have a stronger support among men than among women. There is no noticeable difference in support for the party among different age groups. The support for SD is greater among native born than among foreign born. Since 2014 the SD has substantially increased its support among both foreign-born and foreign-background voters, becoming the third largest party in Sweden also among this demographic by 2017.[^cite_note-svt.se-353] Sympathies are greater for the party among persons with primary and secondary education than among those with a higher education.[^cite_note-SCB2017-354]

A study by *[Aftonbladet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftonbladet)* in 2010 found that 14% of SD members were of immigrant origin,[^cite_note-355] which matches the share of foreign-born inhabitants of Sweden, while their vote share in this population group has always been lower.[^cite_note-356] For the 2010 election in the municipality of [Södertälje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%B6dert%C3%A4lje_Municipality) ([Stockholm County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm_County)), SD was the only party with a majority of immigrants on its electoral list, mostly [Assyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people) from the [Middle East](19323.md).[^cite_note-357] Polling 7.31% (3,447 votes), SD's municipal list in [Södertälje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%B6dert%C3%A4lje_Municipality) got 5 of the 65 municipal seats.[^cite_note-358] Since 2014, the SD has seen growing support from foreign-born Swedish voters, and was estimated to have become the third most popular party for voters of immigrant backgrounds by 2017.[^cite_note-svt.se-353] In recent years, politicians of ethnic minority and immigrant backgrounds have become increasingly active in the party, with notable examples including [Nima Gholam Ali Pour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nima_Gholam_Ali_Pour),[^cite_note-359] [Kent Ekeroth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_Ekeroth), [Sara Gille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Gille)[^cite_note-360] [Arin Karapet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arin_Karapet) and [Rashid Farivar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashid_Farivar).[^cite_note-361]
