### International relations

In its early days, the Sweden Democrats was known to associate itself with both native Swedish and wider European [extreme-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme-right) activist groups and political parties.[^cite_note-vitbok-79][^cite_note-SDBulle_11-89-80] In 1997, the Sweden Democrats made connections with the French National Front (FN) and [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen) after SD spokesman Johan Rinderheim participated in the conference that established Le Pen's *[Euronat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronat)* initiative and the SD received support from the FN in 1998.[^cite_note-366] The SD also founded the *[NordNat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NordNat)* alliance of Nordic nationalist parties in 1997 which also contained the Finnish [Patriotic People's Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriotic_People's_Movement_\(1993\)), the Danish [National Party](https://da.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalpartiet%20Danmark) and the [Fatherland Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatherland_Party_\(Norway\)) of Norway,[^cite_note-367] but otherwise the SD did not actively seek formal relationships outside of Sweden.[^cite_note-vitbok-79][^cite_note-SDBulle_11-89-80] During the late 1990s and early 2000s, the SD began distancing itself from extreme-right parties. The SD broke ties with Euronat in 1998 and NordNat in 1999 and became more influenced by the neighboring [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party) (DF). By the late 2000s the SD said it had ceased regarding the National Front as a role model and instead saw the DF as a sister party.[^cite_note-368] In 2010, party secretary [Björn Söder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_S%C3%B6der) published an article disowning the SD's older connections to extreme or neo-fascist groups or individuals like Le Pen and said SD was more focused on Sweden's issues over interacting with foreign parties.[^cite_note-369] The party has also been active within [counter-jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-jihad) networks, explicitly from 2007 to 2011.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373]

In Europe, SD has had some contacts with the Austrian [FPÖ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_of_Austria), the Dutch [Party for Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_for_Freedom) and [Forum for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forum_for_Democracy), the Flemish [Vlaams Belang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaams_Belang) and the now defunct Belgian [People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Party_\(Belgium\)) over the early 2010s.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] The Danish People's Party was initially indifferent on collaborating with the SD until 2010 when [Pia Kjærsgaard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pia_Kj%C3%A6rsgaard) travelled to Sweden to help with the party's general election campaign.[^cite_note-377] Shortly after, Danish People's Party foreign affairs spokesman [Søren Espersen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%B8ren_Espersen) hosted the SD's conference and said both parties would work together in the [Nordic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Council).[^cite_note-378] Before the [European election of 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_European_Parliament_election_in_Sweden) there was some speculation that the SD would enter a grouping with other European nationalist parties led by [Marine Le Pen](572897.md). SD politicians confirmed they had had met with representatives from the proposed group but said the talks were informal. The Danish People's Party reportedly threatened to end ties with SD if they joined the group, stating that while they were willing to work with the SD and the Dutch Freedom Party, they opposed inclusion of parties like the National Front and the FPÖ.[^cite_note-379] However, after the election the SD began to distance itself from other European far-right parties and elected to become a member of the more moderate [Europe of Freedom and Direct Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_of_Freedom_and_Direct_Democracy) (EFDD) group with the [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party). The SD was also active in the [European Alliance for Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Alliance_for_Freedom) and the [Alliance for Direct Democracy in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_Direct_Democracy_in_Europe) (ADDE) Euro parties with members of the [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party) (UKIP). In 2016, Marine Le Pen stated that the Sweden Democrats were no longer in official cooperation with her party.[^cite_note-380]

In 2015, SD began forging closer relations with the Danish People's Party, and in 2018 announced an official cooperation pact with the [Finns Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finns_Party), which had previously distanced itself from the SD.[^cite_note-nordicpopulist-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] All three parties are members of the [Nordic Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Freedom) group in the [Nordic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Council). Politicians of the Norwegian [Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_Party_\(Norway\)) have called for official collaboration with the SD, although the Progress Party's leadership has traditionally not supported any partnership and turned down joining with the Sweden Democrats on the Nordic Council. In 2022, Progress Party leader [Sylvi Listhaug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvi_Listhaug) officially endorsed the SD during the 2022 Swedish general election and said that while her party shares common ground with the Sweden Democrats on immigration and law & order policies, differences in economic views prevented her from forming a direct alliance with the SD.[^cite_note-nordicpopulist-381][^cite_note-Dagbladet-384]

The SD maintained cooperation with the [Alternative for Germany](38708229.md) party under [Frauke Petry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frauke_Petry)'s leadership when both parties sat with the EFDD group and were members of the [ADDE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_Direct_Democracy_in_Europe) alliance, with Jimmie Åkesson describing the AfD as the SD's "sister party" in Germany during the [2017 German federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_German_federal_election). However, Åkesson has since distanced the SD from statements made by some AfD politicians and by 2024 said the AfD was no longer ideologically compatible with his party.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-auto10-387]

Since 2018, the SD has been a member of the [European Conservatives and Reformists Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Group) (ECR Group) and the [European Conservatives and Reformists Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Party) and presently sits alongside [Brothers of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_of_Italy), the Czech [Civic Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_Democratic_Party_\(Czech_Republic\)), Spanish [Vox](41928488.md) party, the Flemish [N-VA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Flemish_Alliance), Polish [Law and Justice](520309.md), the [Finns Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finns_Party) and [JA21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JA21) from the Netherlands.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] In 2019, there was discussions on whether the SD would join a new group with the Danish People's Party headed by [Matteo Salvini](1767165.md)'s [Lega Nord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lega_Nord). However, Åkesson said SD was not invited to join a new European Parliament group and would stay with the ECR but suggested this may have been due to his party's reluctance to cooperate with Le Pen and pro-Putin parties in Europe.[^cite_note-390] In 2024, the SD sought to distance itself from Hungary's [Fidesz](404647.md) citing what they perceive as [Viktor Orbán](750582.md)'s soft stance on Russia following [its invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and threatened to leave the ECR if Fidesz joined unless Orbán demonstrated a more pro-NATO and Putin-critical position.[^cite_note-391] However, the Sweden Democrats later softened its stance against Fidesz and said it was open to working with the party in the European Parliament, but maintained it would not cooperate with Alternative for Germany and cast doubt on an alliance with the French [National Rally](67119.md).[^cite_note-auto10-387]

In July 2024, the Sweden Democrats formed the European Parliament 'Nordic Freedom' alliance within the ECR along with the [Denmark Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark_Democrats) and the [Finns Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finns_Party), citing common stances on opposing Russian influence, reducing the EU's intervention into the market, and a desire to participate in governments.[^cite_note-392]

Outside of the EU, SD has had informal contacts with the British [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) and the US [Republican Party](32070.md). Individual politicians of the Norwegian [Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_Party_\(Norway\)) have also called for more collaboration with the SD.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394][^cite_note-Dagbladet-384] The SD has also sought to improve and build relations with Israel's [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) which had previously turned down meetings with the SD due to the party's past.[^cite_note-395] In 2024, the SD and Likud began official cooperation with each other.[^cite_note-auto12-328]

## Public profile and perception

### Early image

During the 1980s and early 1990s, several outspoken [far-right](221220.md) and [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism)[^cite_note-svd.se-68][^cite_note-ReferenceA-69][^cite_note-aftonbladet.se-70] advocates were involved with the party.[^cite_note-autogenerated10-153][^cite_note-autogenerated11-396] It was founded by, among others, the Swedish [Waffen-SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waffen-SS) veteran [Gustaf Ekström](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustaf_Ekstr%C3%B6m)[^cite_note-svd.se-68][^cite_note-ReferenceA-69][^cite_note-aftonbladet.se-70] and members of both older Nazi and [neo-Nazi](54361.md) organisations. In its early days, the SD also had a reputation for attracting biker and skinhead gangs to its rallies before disconnecting itself with neo-Nazi and skinhead movements in the mid-1990s. The party had flyers printed by the [French National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_National_Front) in the 1998 general election,[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] and was financially backed for the 2004 European election by Belgian businessman and racial conspiracy theorist [Bernard Mengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Mengal?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] In the 1990s, the party was a member of the *[Euronat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronat)* initiative which was set up by [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen) before severing ties with it in 1999.[^cite_note-401] In 1999, the party program was updated to state its opposition to Nazism: "The Sweden Democrats reject with the same contempt Nazism, whose leitmotif is the Fuhrer principle, racial superiority and aggressive war, and which can be seen as a mirror image of and a reaction to Marxism." In 2005, the SD acknowledged that it had contained people with extreme political views at the time of its establishment but denied that it was founded as a neo-Nazi party or had ever endorsed fascist and national socialist ideas in its manifesto.[^cite_note-402] As part of the moderation process, the Sweden Democrats began expelling extreme far-right members from the early 2000s onwards and in 2010 updated the party constitution to include clauses against racism, extremism and criminal behaviour. Since the 2010s, the SD has also sought to distance itself from far-right parties in Europe, including turning down an invitation to join an alliance in the [European Parliament](9581.md) with the French National Front in 2014 and choosing to ally themselves with more moderate parties during the 2014 and 2019 European elections.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404]

In June 2025, the SD leadership issued an official apology for the party's historical links to neo-fascist and neo-Nazi figures and groups, with SD spokesman [Mattias Karlsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattias_Karlsson_\(politician,_born_1977\)) stating "That there have been clear expressions of antisemitism and support for National Socialist ideas in my party's history, I think, is disgusting and reprehensible. I would like to reiterate the party's apology, above all to Swedish citizens of Jewish descent who may have felt a strong sense of insecurity and fear for good reasons."[^cite_note-405]

### Isolation in parliament

Both before and after the [2010 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Swedish_general_election) all the major parties, including the [Swedish Social Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Social_Democrats) and the [Moderate Party](202985.md), declared they would not collaborate or work with the Sweden Democrats. The move was described by international pundits as an unofficial *[cordon sanitaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordon_sanitaire_\(politics\))*.[^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] The policy of non-cooperation was kept in place for the [2014 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Swedish_general_election). Following the 2018 general election, which saw the disintegration of the centre-right [Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_\(Sweden\)), Christian Democrats leader [Ebba Busch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebba_Busch) and Moderates leader [Ulf Kristersson](3246360.md) signaled an end to the non-cooperation policy and began talks with the SD. The policy of non-cooperation was officially scrapped by the Moderates, Christian Democrats and The Liberals for the 2022 election when all four parties signed the [Tidö Agreement](72007581.md).[^cite_note-auto5-130][^cite_note-auto3-131]

### Media boycotts

The Sweden Democrats have complained about difficulties buying advertising space before elections due to the media banning the party from advertising and have accused media outlets of political bias and trying to censor or limit the party's campaign messages during elections.[^cite_note-408] This has been criticised by free speech organisations in Sweden and abroad.[^cite_note-409] On 16 June 2006, *[Dagens Nyheter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dagens_Nyheter)* and *[Svenska Dagbladet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svenska_Dagbladet)* decided to stop their boycott. *[Expressen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressen)*, however, still retains a ban on Sweden Democrat advertising.[^cite_note-410] During the [2010 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Swedish_general_election), broadcaster [TV4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV4_\(Swedish_TV_channel\)) refused to air a Sweden Democrats campaign video which depicted a Swedish pensioner being outrun by [burka](165826.md)-clad women with [prams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_transport).[^cite_note-aljazeera-411][^cite_note-412] TV4's decision was criticized by both free speech advocates and politicians from Denmark, including by [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party) leader [Pia Kjærsgaard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pia_Kj%C3%A6rsgaard), [Venstre](403109.md) and the [Conservative People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_People's_Party_\(Denmark\)) (the latter of whom reacted to TV4's decision to ban the video by calling for international election observers to be sent to Sweden), and by members of the Norwegian [Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_Party_\(Norway\)) who called the decision a "violation of democratic rules". The SD subsequently uploaded the video to [YouTube](3524766.md) where it was viewed over a million times.[^cite_note-413] Swedish political journalist Hanne Kjöller argued that attempts to censor the SD in 2010 by the media and left-wing protest groups ultimately backfired and ended up [emboldening their support by giving them more publicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streisand_effect).[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-aft-415][^cite_note-416]

### Muhammad cartoon debate

After the Danish daily newspaper *[Jyllands-Posten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten)* published twelve cartoons depicting [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad) and ignited [a controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten_Muhammad_cartoons_controversy) during the 2005 autumn and winter, the Sweden Democrats gave their unreserved support to the publication with reference to the [freedom of speech](21401843.md). SD stated that it saw no reason why a Danish newspaper should be forced to abide by Muslim rules and prohibitions regarding expression. When the boycott of Danish products was launched in the Middle East, SD launched a "Buy Danish" campaign in support of Danish workers.[^cite_note-417] In 2006 SD entered [the Muhammad cartoon debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten_Muhammad_cartoons_controversy) by publishing a cartoon depicting Muhammad on its youth league (SDU) and *SD-Kuriren* (pronounced [\[ˈɛ̂sːdeːkɵˌriːrɛn\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Swedish) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/b/b1/Sv-SD-Kuriren.ogg/Sv-SD-Kuriren.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/b/b1/Sv-SD-Kuriren.ogg/Sv-SD-Kuriren.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>) websites. The cartoon showed Muhammad from behind holding a mirror in front of his face. However, instead of any facial features, the mirror showed only a blank head. The cartoon was captioned "Muhammad's Face" (Swedish: *Muhammeds ansikte* [\[mɵˈhǎmːɛds ˈânːsɪktɛ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Swedish) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/0/0c/Sv-Muhammeds_ansikte.ogg/Sv-Muhammeds_ansikte.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/0/0c/Sv-Muhammeds_ansikte.ogg/Sv-Muhammeds_ansikte.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>).[^cite_note-autogenerated44-418]

The publication attracted the attention of the Swedish government, which informed internet service provider *Levonline* about the SD's publications. Subsequently, *Levonline* shut down SD's web page. The [Minister for Foreign Affairs](462050.md), [Laila Freivalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laila_Freivalds), denied any direct interference. However, at the same time, Laila Freivalds condemned the publication as a provocation.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] Freivalds then resigned from the [Persson Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persson_Cabinet) after being accused of interference with press freedom and lying about such actions.

This event spurred debate on government censorship in Sweden. The Sweden Democrats also had a [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech) charge filed against them due to the posted caricature.[^cite_note-423] Similar hate speech charges were filed against other Swedish publishers who had depicted Muhammad.[^cite_note-autogenerated44-418] However, these charges were immediately deemed to be unfounded by the [Swedish Chancellor of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chancellor_of_Justice_\(Sweden\)).[^cite_note-424]

The Sweden Democrats originally planned to publish a set of cartoons in their newspaper *SD-Kuriren*. However, after the controversy erupted, Jimmie Åkesson issued a statement on SD's website on 9 February 2006, stating that they would refrain from further publications online and in print, due to concerns that publishing might spur hostile actions against Swedes and Swedish interests.[^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

The shutdown of the Sweden Democrats' websites was reported to the [Committee on the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_on_the_Constitution) by the [Liberal People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberals_\(Sweden\)) leader [Lars Leijonborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_Leijonborg).[^cite_note-428] SD filed complaints against the [Security Service](368897.md) (*Säpo*) and the [Ministry for Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_for_Foreign_Affairs_\(Sweden\)) with the *Justitiekansler* and *[Justitieombudsmannen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_Ombudsman)*, alleging that the government's interference was unconstitutional.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] The spokesperson of the [Green Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Party_\(Sweden\)), [Peter Eriksson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Eriksson_\(politician\)), also criticised the involvement of the [Ministry for Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_for_Foreign_Affairs_\(Sweden\)) in the event.[^cite_note-431]

### Racist incidents and expulsions

The Sweden Democrats have, among all Swedish parliamentary parties, had the largest share of elected municipal representatives resign since the 2010 elections (27.8%).[^cite_note-432] Many of these resignations were brought on by racist statements or actions by these representatives.[^cite_note-\:0-349]

In November 2012, party spokesperson [Erik Almqvist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erik_Almqvist) resigned after he had been caught on tape making racist and sexist statements.[^cite_note-433] In November 2012, parliamentarian [Lars Isovaara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_Isovaara) resigned after accusing two people of foreign origin of stealing his bag (which Isovaara had left at a restaurant) and then proceeding to verbally abuse a security guard of a foreign background.[^cite_note-434] Isovaara's replacement in parliament, [Markus Wiechel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Wiechel), was found in April 2013 to have referred to a group of black people as "monkeys" in a Facebook comment back in 2011. Wiechel later apologised and stated the comment had been in reference to a video of a tribal witch burning in Africa.[^cite_note-435]

In March 2013, 12 individuals were thrown out of the party for their involvement in [neo-Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism_in_Sweden) or other extremist movements.[^cite_note-436] In November 2013, parliamentarian and then vice party leader [Jonas Åkerlund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_%C3%85kerlund_\(politician\)) gained attention for having called immigrants "parasites" during a broadcast on SD's own radio station in 2002, after the recording was publicly rediscovered. In his defence, Åkerlund stated that he only said it to provoke people.[^cite_note-437] In September 2014, the party chairman of the local [Stockholm](26741.md) branch, [Christoffer Dulny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christoffer_Dulny) was asked to resign from his position after it was found he had previously posted mocking comments about immigrants, calling them "shameless liars" on [alternative media sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_media).[^cite_note-438] He also resigned from parliament on the same day.[^cite_note-439]

In October 2016, a video of the parliamentarian and economic policy spokesperson [Oscar Sjöstedt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Sj%C3%B6stedt) laughing at [antisemitic](1078.md) jokes was released by a former school friend of his who also accused Sjöstedt of chanting fascist slogans. Whilst at a party believed to have been organized by the neo-Nazi group *[Info-14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Info-14)* in 2011 when Sjöstedt was a member of the SD's youth wing, he laughingly told a story about former co-workers with [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism) sympathies mocking Jews and comparing them to sheep. Following an investigation by the SD, Sjöstedt stated that a friend had invited him to the party but he had walked out upon discovering who had organized it and denied expressing fascist statements.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442] During the same month, the parliamentarian and second deputy party leader [Carina Herrstedt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carina_St%C3%A5hl_Herrstedt) was confronted with having sent an allegedly [racist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism), [antisemitic](1078.md), [homophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobia) and [anti-romanyist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Romanyism) email to her then spouse in 2011. The email, which had been leaked from the party's internal servers, for instance contained phrases that referred to black [football](10568.md) players from the team [Landskrona BoIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landskrona_BoIS) as "[negroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negro)" whilst also picturing [Romani people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) as thieves. The email was meant to be playful and ironic, Herrstedt said to [Aftonbladet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftonbladet).[^cite_note-443]

Between 2015 and 2016, various members of the party were expelled from the SD for expressing extremist or racist views,[^cite_note-thelocal2-444][^cite_note-SVR2-445][^cite_note-Heinisch-446] or because of disagreement with the party's shift towards [moderation](39619035.md) and [social conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_conservatism).[^cite_note-Heinisch-446] In April 2015, the [Sweden Democratic Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_Democratic_Youth) leaders were also expelled for these reasons,[^cite_note-thelocal2-444][^cite_note-Heinisch-446] and the organisation was dissolved shortly after with the mother party issuing a warning for remaining SDU members to leave the youth wing or be expelled from the party.[^cite_note-Heinisch-446] In December 2016, the parliamentarian [Anna Hagwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Hagwall) was thrown out of the party after using arguments associated with [antisemitism](1078.md) to argue for a bill that she introduced in parliament intended to reduce [concentration of media ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_of_media_ownership) in Sweden.[^cite_note-SVR2-445][^cite_note-447]

In September 2017, a report from *Dagens ETC* found that 14 former municipal representatives of the party had infiltrated the SD in order to financially support the [Nordic Resistance Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Resistance_Movement),[^cite_note-ETC1-448][^cite_note-ETC2-449] a neo-Nazi organisation, through [financial transactions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_transaction),[^cite_note-ETC1-448][^cite_note-ETC2-449] memberships,[^cite_note-ETC1-448][^cite_note-ETC2-449] or purchases of antisemitic and racist literature or souvenirs.[^cite_note-ETC1-448][^cite_note-ETC2-449] In August 2018, 2 members were kicked out due to purchases of [Nazi memorabilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_memorabilia) online;[^cite_note-SVR3-450] following the expulsions, Michael Erlandsson, one of the SD's spokespeople,[^cite_note-SVR3-450] publicly stated that people who "have these types of views and share these types of materials" have no place in the party and that the SD maintains a zero-tolerance stance on expressing fascist views.[^cite_note-SVR3-450] 14 candidates were expelled from the party as well after being exposed as former members of neo-Nazi organisations.[^cite_note-Expressen-451] Referring to the latest expulsions, SD leader [Jimmie Åkesson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmie_%C3%85kesson) declared that the party "works extremely hard to keep clean".[^cite_note-Expressen-451]

In 2017, Martin Strid, party spokesman in [Borlänge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borl%C3%A4nge), appeared to state at a televised SD rally that Muslims were not "fully human" and humane. Strid's comments were met with condemnation within the SD. Åkesson and SD party secretary [Richard Jomshof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Jomshof) described them as racist, with Jomshof stating the SD advocates ideological criticism, but not violating human rights. SD board member [Aron Emilsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aron_Emilsson) said that Strid would face a disciplinary meeting for violating the party's code of conduct. In response, Strid said he had expressed himself "clumsily" but chose to quit the party after being given an ultimatum to resign or be expelled.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453]

In March 2022, parliamentarian [Roger Richthoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Richthoff) was sacked from his role as party defense spokesman and subsequently expelled from the SD and banned by the party from standing as a candidate for them in the [2022 Swedish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Swedish_general_election) after posting controversial comments on [Twitter](9988187.md), including sharing a video supporting Russia in the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md) in which several [antisemitic conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitic_conspiracy_theories) were presented.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] In December 2025, parliamentarian [Göran Hargestam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6ran_Hargestam) resigned his seat after a scandal in which he was found to have kept a collection of [Nazi memorabilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_memorabilia) in an office in [Valdemarsvik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valdemarsvik).[^cite_note-456]

Researcher on Nordic nationalism [Benjamin R. Teitelbaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_R._Teitelbaum) described the present day version Sweden Democrats as paradoxical compared to other European nationalist parties on the issues of racism and radicalism among its members. Teitelbaum notes that in contrast to other Nordic and wider European populist parties, the SD differs by having a past rooted in white nationalism and extremism, but in the present day is comparably more proactive in rejecting ethno-nationalism and expelling members who make racist statements to the point where he considers the SD to be on the "softer side" of national-populism.[^cite_note-auto7-158] Similar observations were made by British conservative author [Douglas Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Murray_\(author\)) who described the SD as undergoing one of the most significant transformations on the European political right from a party on the fringes that openly pushed extreme tendencies to a more mainstream movement that draws on diverse support.[^cite_note-Murray-457] Kateřina Lišaníková argued that out of all the parties in Sweden, the SD was more likely to attract individual members who made extreme statements regarding immigrants but this was a consequence of the SD being the only party in the Riksdag willing to challenge the political consensus on immigration for several years as opposed to the SD itself being an extremist or racist party.[^cite_note-\:4-172]

[Ashley Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashley_Fox), leader of the [British Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) [MEPs](258909.md), praised the Sweden Democrats regarding the party's policy decisions on the expulsion of extremist and racist members:[^cite_note-Independent2-14] "Over the past decade the Sweden Democrats have made progress in reforming themselves, expelling any members displaying unacceptable views or behaviour and diversifying their party base."[^cite_note-Independent2-14]

### Iron pipe scandal

Main article: [Iron pipe scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_pipe_scandal)

On November 14, 2012, Erik Almqvist, in addition to party spokesperson [Kent Ekeroth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_Ekeroth) and party official Christian Westling were filmed arming themselves with iron pipes before they sought out a confrontation with [Soran Ismail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soran_Ismail), a Swedish comedian of [Kurdish](17068.md) descent.[^cite_note-458]

### Splinter parties

Since the SD's inception, breakaway parties have been formed by former SD members, many of whom were either removed from the party due to controversial actions or resigned after the SD began to ideologically shift and moderate itself.

In 1995, former SD spokesman [Leif Zeilon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leif_Zeilon) established the [Homeland Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(Sweden\)) along with other founding members of the SD after Anders Klarström was replaced as party chairman by a less hardline leadership.[^cite_note-Så_ska_de_slå_ut_SD-459]

In 2001, a major breakaway occurred when the more radical ethno-nationalist faction of the SD's national board and their supporters were collectively expelled and formed the [National Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democrats_\(Sweden\)) party.[^cite_note-Så_ska_de_slå_ut_SD-459]

On 18 March 2018, [Alternative for Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_for_Sweden) (AfS) was formed by former members of the SD's old youth wing after they were kicked out of the party after alleged ties with extremist groups and for coming into conflict with the mother party. Three SD parliamentarians joined Alternative for Sweden but all lost their seats during the 2018 general election.[^cite_note-\:7-460] AfS's policies have been criticised as too extreme by members of the SD such as [Henrik Vinge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henrik_Vinge).[^cite_note-\:7-460]

### Lobbying

The Sweden Democrats came under fire in 2015 for changing their position on profits made by private welfare companies. Before the [election in 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Swedish_general_election) they favored having restrictions on the amount of profit that welfare companies could take and use for their own gain. Since the election, they have favored the approach of [the Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_\(Sweden\)) parties, that is higher and more restrictive quality standards.[^cite_note-461] This has been suspected to be because of extensive [lobbying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobbying) done by the organisation [Svenskt Näringsliv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederation_of_Swedish_Enterprise) among others. The story was discovered by the Swedish newspaper *[Dagens Industri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dagens_Industri)* on 14 September 2015.[^cite_note-462] SD has denied all accusations of corruption.

### Anonymous social media accounts

On May 7, 2024, one month prior to the [2024 EU elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election_in_Sweden), the [TV4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV4_\(Swedish_TV_channel\)) investigative journalism program *Kalla fakta* ('Hard Facts') premiered a two-part documentary (*[Undercover i trollfabriken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undercover_i_trollfabriken?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, 'Undercover in the Troll Factory')[^cite_note-463] revealing that the Sweden Democrats systematically used anonymous accounts on [social media](5897742.md) platforms such as [TikTok](56822861.md), [X](9988187.md) and [Facebook](7529378.md). The scoop was obtained through the use of the [wallraff method](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BCnter_Wallraff) where a reporter spent five months as an employee at the previously SD-owned YouTube channel Riks as well as the party's communications department,[^cite_note-464] documenting internal discussions on the party's communication strategies. The documentary revealed that at least 23 anonymous social media accounts were actually run from SD's communications department, spreading [xenophobic](34141.md) content and satirical attacks on opposing politicians from other parties, including [deepfake](56641018.md) videos. According to *Kalla fakta*, posts from these accounts reached 27 million views across social media platforms.[^cite_note-465] SD's head of communication, Joakim Wallerstein, repeated earlier claims that the party does not run any anonymous social media accounts. However, according to Swedish newspaper [Aftonbladet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftonbladet), internal communication reveals that Wallerstein already in 2012 wanted to include anonymous internet campaigns as part of the party's strategy.[^cite_note-466]

In an official response from the Sweden Democrats' leader Jimmie Åkesson, the *Kalla fakta* documentary was dismissed as a "gigantic, domestic influence operation by the left-liberal establishment".[^cite_note-467] In numerous interviews, SD representatives toned down the content posted on the anonymous accounts as simple satire and humor material.[^cite_note-468] Following the documentary, and the party's response to it, SD received massive criticism from all other Swedish parliamentary parties.[^cite_note-469] The government parties accused SD of not following the [Tidö Agreement](72007581.md), which contains a clause about showing respect towards other parties. After a meeting on May 16, SD agreed that some of the anonymous accounts' posts went against the agreement and agreed to delete 45 posts. They did not agree to stop running anonymous accounts but said they would use a softer tone towards parties in the Tidö coalition.[^cite_note-470]
